MED13 Syndrome

Mendelian MONDO:0032485 Pathograph 46 Show in embeddings browser Autosomal dominant intellectual disability Neurodevelopmental disorder CDK8-kinase module-associated disorder

MED13 syndrome (MRD61) is a rare autosomal dominant neurodevelopmental disorder caused by heterozygous pathogenic variants in MED13, which encodes mediator complex subunit 13. The Mediator complex is a multi-subunit transcriptional coactivator that bridges gene-specific transcription factors to RNA polymerase II. MED13 is part of the CDK8 kinase module, which reversibly associates with the Mediator core. Pathogenic MED13 variants disrupt transcriptional regulation in developing tissues, leading to intellectual disability, speech and language delay, variable congenital heart defects, facial dysmorphism, hypotonia, and behavioral abnormalities including autism spectrum disorder and ADHD. To date, only approximately 26 cases have been reported worldwide. Both truncating (haploinsufficiency) and clustered missense variants (affecting a conserved phosphodegron at Thr326/Pro327) have been identified. The phenotypic spectrum continues to expand with subsequent case reports, which have added structural brain anomalies (hydrocephalus, optic chiasm and brainstem atrophy), skeletal and limb abnormalities, restricted growth, dental anomalies (supernumerary teeth), and rare enteric involvement (congenital aganglionic megacolon).

Inheritance

1
Autosomal Dominant HP:0000006
Autosomal dominant, with variants predominantly arising de novo. Two departures from a simple dominant model matter for counselling. First, penetrance is incomplete: a pathogenic MED13 variant has been documented in an affected proband inherited from an asymptomatic parent, in whom the variant had itself arisen de novo. Second, vertical transmission from an affected parent is documented in at least two families. A variant should therefore not be dismissed because a parent carries it and is unaffected, and parental testing is warranted even when the phenotype looks sporadic.
Autosomal dominant inheritance Penetrance: INCOMPLETE Expressivity: VARIABLE
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:38453051 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The patients carried different variants (1 splice-site variant, 4 nonsense and 7 frameshift) in 11 genes: CAMTA1, MBD5, KMT2C, KMT2E, ZMIZ1, MN1, NDUFB11, CUL3, MED13, ARID2 and RERE."
MED13 appears in a systematically assembled series of neurodevelopmental-disorder genes with a pathogenic variant inherited from an asymptomatic parent.
PMID:38453051 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Incomplete penetrance for SNV and MNV in neurodevelopmental disorders might be more frequent than previously thought. This point is crucial to consider for interpretation of variants, family investigation, genetic counseling, and prenatal diagnosis."
States the counselling implication of incomplete penetrance for genes such as MED13 that are usually treated as fully penetrant de novo disorders.
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Here we describe a GeneMatcher collaboration which led to a cohort of 13 affected individuals harboring protein-altering variants, 11 of which are de novo, in MED13; the only inherited variant was transmitted to an affected child from an affected mother."
Establishes de novo predominance with documented vertical transmission.
◈

Mechanistic Hypotheses

4
MED13 haploinsufficiency as the mechanism for truncating alleles
med13_haploinsufficiency CANONICAL Truncating (nonsense / frameshift / whole-gene deletion)
Evidence balance 2 support
Heterozygous truncating MED13 variants and whole-gene 17q23.2 deletions reduce the pool of CDK8 kinase module available to dock on core Mediator. Supported by the predominance of de novo truncating alleles in the founding cohort, by an independent whole-gene deletion phenocopy, and by the explicit haploinsufficiency conclusion drawn from a functionally characterised frameshift case.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41561257 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This case is also consistent with haploinsufficiency as the disease mechanism for truncating MED13 variants in MRD61, underscoring the importance of exome sequencing in patients with neurodevelopmental disorders and congenital anomalies."
States the haploinsufficiency conclusion for truncating alleles directly.
PMID:22162340 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"we suggest that MED13 haploinsufficiency was responsible for the phenotype of the patient particularly the cataract, hearing loss and semicircular canal dysplasia"
Independent whole-gene dosage-loss support: a 17q23.2 microdeletion encompassing MED13 reproduces a MED13-attributable phenotype.
Clustered missense variants act by altered function rather than simple dosage loss
med13_missense_altered_function EMERGING Missense (N-terminal degron, central 834/835, C-terminal clusters)
Evidence balance 3 support 1 refute
Pathogenic MED13 missense variants cluster in two small regions — an N-terminal region containing the Thr326/Pro327 SCF-Fbw7 phosphodegron and a C-terminal region — plus a central pair of residues around 834/835. The clustering itself argues against simple loss of function, which would tolerate variants anywhere. Clinically, the most severe reported presentations (developmental and epileptic encephalopathy, neonatal multi-organ disease with mitochondrial pathology) have so far occurred with missense rather than truncating alleles. Degron-region variants are predicted to stabilise MED13 against Fbw7-mediated turnover, which would raise rather than lower CKM occupancy. This is structured inference from position and phenotype; no functional assay has yet demonstrated altered function for any MED13 missense allele.
Kept as EMERGING rather than CANONICAL, and explicitly contested. The clustering and severity signals are real, but no functional data distinguish altered function from a severe hypomorph, ClinGen's expert-panel curation across the full allelic series concluded loss of function, and the same p.Pro835Ser allele has been reported with quite different clinical courses.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Out of the seven non-truncating mutations, six clustered in two specific locations of the MED13 protein: an N-terminal and C-terminal region."
Establishes the positional clustering that is the primary argument for a non-loss-of-function missense mechanism.
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The four N-terminal clustering mutations affect two adjacent amino acids that are known to be involved in MED13 ubiquitination and degradation, p.Thr326 and p.Pro327."
Localises the N-terminal cluster to the degradation signal, the basis for the protein-stabilisation prediction.
PMID:41130977 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This is the first case report linking a MED13 variant to systemic mitochondrial dysfunction, suggesting a novel pathogenic mechanism."
A central-region missense allele produced a severe multi-organ phenotype the authors themselves flag as mechanistically novel.
+ 1 more reference
Fbw7-degron stabilisation as a dosage-restoration strategy
med13_fbw7_degron_stabilisation EMERGING
Evidence balance 1 support 1 refute
If SCF-Fbw7-mediated turnover sets the steady-state level of MED13 protein, then slowing turnover of the product of the intact allele in a haploinsufficient cell could raise MED13 toward the diploid level and restore CKM occupancy. This is the one route from MED13 mechanism to a candidate therapeutic handle. The major caveat is target selectivity: FBXW7 is a broadly acting tumour suppressor, so non-selective inhibition of its ligase activity carries oncogenic risk and any viable strategy would need to spare its other substrates.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23322298 SUPPORT In Vitro
"MED13/13L physically link the CDK8 module to Mediator, and Fbw7 loss increases CDK8 module-Mediator association."
Demonstrates that reducing Fbw7 activity raises CKM-Mediator association, the effect the strategy would exploit.
PMID:17909001 REFUTE Human Clinical
"Our results indicate that FBXW7 is inactivated by mutation in diverse human cancer types with an overall mutation frequency of approximately 6%."
Counter-evidence establishing the oncogenic risk of reducing FBXW7 function, the principal safety objection to this strategy.
The cardiac miR-208a/MED13 metabolic axis as a systemic-phenotype modifier
med13_mir208a_metabolic_axis EMERGING
Evidence balance 2 support
In mice, cardiac MED13 acts through nuclear-receptor signalling to control systemic energy expenditure: cardiac MED13 overexpression or miR-208a inhibition confers resistance to diet-induced obesity, while cardiomyocyte MED13 deletion worsens it. If this axis is dosage-sensitive in humans, it offers a candidate explanation for the restricted growth reported in a subset of MRD61 patients that is independent of the neurodevelopmental phenotype, and predicts that variant class might track with growth and cardiac trajectory. No human data test this.
Entirely murine. The human MRD61 growth phenotype is restricted growth rather than obesity, so the direction of any human effect is not established; this is a hypothesis about a candidate modifier axis, not a claimed mechanism.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:22541436 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Cardiac-specific overexpression of MED13 or pharmacologic inhibition of miR-208a in mice confers resistance to high-fat diet-induced obesity and improves systemic insulin sensitivity and glucose tolerance."
Establishes the bidirectional, dosage-dependent link from cardiac MED13 level to systemic metabolism.
PMID:22541436 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Conversely, genetic deletion of MED13 specifically in cardiomyocytes enhances obesity in response to high-fat diet and exacerbates metabolic syndrome."
The loss-of-function direction, which is the one relevant to MED13 haploinsufficiency in patients.
⚙

Pathophysiology

11
CKM hinge anchoring by MED13
MED13 is the hinge subunit that physically tethers the four-protein CDK8 kinase module (CKM: MED13, MED12, CDK8, cyclin C) to core Mediator. Structural work shows MED13 adopts an Argonaute-like bi-lobal fold, and that a large intrinsically disordered region within it forms a principal CKM-core interface which directly occludes the RNA polymerase II and MED26 binding sites — making CKM docking a kinase-independent repressor of Mediator-dependent transcription. Division of labour within the module is asymmetric: MED12 activates CDK8 kinase activity, whereas MED13 mediates the association of the module with Mediator. This node is what MED13 dosage and structural integrity actually control, and is the mechanistic layer the previous version of this entry summarised only as "connective role".

Downstream
CKM occupancy of core Mediator and bidirectional transcriptional control MED13 abundance and integrity set how much CKM is docked on core Mediator.
Disrupted Mediator complex transcriptional regulation Loss of the anchoring subunit is the proximal cause of dysregulated Mediator-dependent transcription.
Stress-induced Med13 degradation and cyclin C release Because MED13 is the nuclear anchor for cyclin C as well as the CKM hinge, a reduced MED13 pool is predicted to sit closer to the threshold at which stress releases cyclin C to the cytoplasm.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:39321804 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The CKM binds to multiple regions on cMED through both MED12 and MED13, including a large intrinsically disordered region (IDR) in the latter."
Cryo-EM of the complete human Mediator identifies the MED13 intrinsically disordered region as a principal CKM-core interface.
PMID:39321804 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Notably, the MED13 IDR obstructs the recruitment of RNA Pol II/MED26 onto cMED by direct occlusion of their respective binding sites, leading to functional repression of cMED-dependent transcription."
Defines the structural mechanism of kinase-independent repression that MED13 dosage controls.
PMID:33523904 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Unexpectedly, Med13 has a characteristic Argonaute-like bi-lobal architecture."
Establishes the structural fold of MED13.
+ 1 more reference
CKM occupancy of core Mediator and bidirectional transcriptional control
CKM docking on core Mediator sterically blocks RNA polymerase II recruitment, and this repression is independent of CDK8 catalytic activity — MED12 and MED13, not kinase activity, are the critical subunits for it. The module is nevertheless not a pure repressor: CDK8 also acts as a context-dependent co-activator across the p53, Wnt/beta-catenin, serum-response, SMAD and thyroid-hormone-receptor programs. Consequently a perturbation of MED13 dosage dysregulates transcription bidirectionally at CKM-gated promoters and enhancers rather than simply de-repressing them, which is why MED13-related disease is a transcriptional dysregulation disorder and not a straightforward loss of repression.

PMID:17043218 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Here, we report that the ability of the Cdk8 module to prevent pol II interactions is independent of the Cdk8-dependent kinase activity."
Establishes that CKM repression is steric and kinase-independent, so it depends on module presence — that is, on MED13.
PMID:19240132 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Using a reconstituted transcription system together with recombinant or endogenous CDK8 subcomplexes, we demonstrate that, in fact, Med12 and Med13 are critical for subcomplex-dependent repression, whereas CDK8 kinase activity is not."
Names MED13 specifically as a subunit required for repression independently of kinase activity.
PMID:19240132 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Structural and biochemical studies confirm the CDK8 submodule binds the Mediator leg/tail domain via the Med13 subunit, and this submodule-Mediator association precludes pol II recruitment."
Confirms MED13 as the physical point of attachment for the module.
+ 1 more reference
Disrupted Mediator complex transcriptional regulation
MED13 is a subunit of the CDK8 kinase module of the Mediator complex, which regulates RNA polymerase II-dependent transcription. MED13 plays a connective role between the CDK8 kinase module and the Mediator core. Pathogenic variants disrupt Mediator-dependent transcriptional control and broader gene-expression programs. MED13 belongs to the group of CDK8-kinase module-associated disease genes, alongside MED12, MED13L, and CDK8.

Downstream
Neurodevelopmental transcriptional dysregulation Mediator dysfunction perturbs neuronal developmental gene-expression programs that contribute to developmental delay, language impairment, and intellectual disability.
Cardiac developmental transcriptional dysregulation Mediator dysfunction can also perturb cardiac developmental transcriptional programs, contributing to variable congenital heart anomalies in a subset of patients.
Craniofacial, ocular, and sensory developmental dysregulation Because the Mediator complex is required for transcription of nearly all RNA polymerase II genes, MED13 dysfunction also perturbs developmental programs in neural-crest- and placode-derived tissues, contributing to facial dysmorphism, ocular motility and optic-nerve anomalies, sensorineural hearing loss, dental anomalies, and rare enteric (neural-crest) involvement.
Somatic growth and skeletal developmental dysregulation Broad transcriptional pleiotropy of Mediator dysfunction extends to somatic growth and skeletal/limb developmental programs in a subset of patients.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"MED13 is a component of the CDK8-kinase module that can reversibly bind Mediator, a multi-protein complex that is required for Polymerase II transcription initiation. Mutations in several other genes encoding subunits of Mediator have been previously shown to associate with DD/ID, including..."
The foundational paper establishes MED13 as a CDK8-kinase module disease gene and describes its role in Mediator-dependent transcription.
PMID:33390853 SUPPORT Other
"MED13 plays a connective role between the CKM and the MED core"
Review article describes MED13's structural role connecting the CDK8 kinase module to the Mediator core complex.
PMID:29325037 SUPPORT Model Organism
"MED13 is essential for ZGA in the mouse, in part by regulating expression of the embryo-specific chromatin remodeling complex, esBAF."
Mouse study establishes MED13 as an essential regulator of early developmental transcription (zygotic genome activation), with its paralog MED13L only partially compensating, supporting the dosage sensitivity of MED13-dependent transcription in development.
MED13 phosphodegron disruption and impaired protein turnover
Several pathogenic missense variants cluster at a conserved phosphodegron (Thr326/Pro327) in MED13. This phosphodegron is normally recognized by the SCF-Fbw7 ubiquitin ligase complex, leading to MED13 ubiquitination and proteasomal degradation. Mutations at these residues may impair MED13 protein turnover, potentially resulting in gain-of-function or dominant-negative effects through accumulation of mutant protein.

Downstream
Disrupted Mediator complex transcriptional regulation Impaired SCF-Fbw7-mediated MED13 turnover can alter CDK8-module availability at the Mediator complex and intensify transcriptional dysregulation.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The four N-terminal clustering mutations affect two adjacent amino acids that are known to be involved in MED13 ubiquitination and degradation, p.Thr326 and p.Pro327."
Snijders Blok et al. identified clustering of missense variants at residues involved in MED13 protein turnover, suggesting disrupted ubiquitin-mediated degradation as a disease mechanism.
PMID:33390853 SUPPORT Other
"Normally, this phosphodegron is recognized by the S-phase kinase-associated protein (Skp)-Cullin-F-box (SCF) F-box and WD repeat domain-containing 7 (Fbw7) ubiquitin ligase complex (SCFFbw7), resulting in MED13 protein ubiquitination and degradation"
Review details the SCF-Fbw7-dependent degradation mechanism for MED13, providing molecular context for why phosphodegron mutations are pathogenic.
Neurodevelopmental transcriptional dysregulation
Downstream of MED13/Mediator dysfunction, neuronal gene-expression programs are dysregulated during brain development, contributing to intellectual disability, global developmental delay, language impairment, and variable behavioral phenotypes. Mechanistic studies in mice indicate that MED13 is required cell-autonomously for cortical neuron development: knockdown of Med13 in cortical neurons impairs radial migration, contralateral axonal projection, and dendritic complexity, in part through PlxnA4, linking MED13 dysfunction to the neurodevelopmental disorder phenotype.

Intellectual disability Disrupted neuronal developmental programs contribute to the core cognitive impairment phenotype.
Global developmental delay Early developmental milestone acquisition is impaired downstream of neuronal transcriptional dysregulation.
Delayed speech and language development Language acquisition is especially sensitive to the disrupted neurodevelopmental program in MED13-related disorder.
Autism spectrum disorder Variable downstream neurobehavioral manifestations can include autistic features in a subset of affected individuals.
Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder Variable downstream neurobehavioral manifestations can include ADHD in a subset of affected individuals.
Hypotonia Disrupted neurodevelopmental programs contribute to muscular hypotonia, particularly during infancy, which compounds motor delay.
Motor delay Disrupted neurodevelopmental programs contribute to delayed acquisition of motor milestones, a common feature distinct from hypotonia and from global developmental delay.
Speech apraxia Disruption of motor-speech programming networks produces apraxia in a subset, distinct from the general speech delay affecting the whole cohort.
Severe developmental language disorder The language-dominant character of the phenotype, sufficient for MED13 to be recovered through developmental-language-disorder ascertainment alone.
Impaired brain structural development and network excitability Dysregulated neuronal developmental transcription also disturbs brain morphogenesis and cortical network formation, the substrate for structural brain anomalies and seizures.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41663567 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We found that silencing Med13 in cortical neurons impaired its radial migration and contralateral projection as well as dendritic complexity in mice."
Mouse in-utero electroporation knockdown shows MED13 is required for cortical neuron migration and connectivity, a mechanism linking MED13 loss to the neurodevelopmental phenotype.
Impaired brain structural development and network excitability
Dysregulation of MED13/Mediator-dependent neurodevelopmental transcription disturbs brain growth, midline and cortical morphogenesis, and the balance of neuronal network excitability. This contributes to structural brain anomalies (microcephaly, corpus callosum abnormalities, and in severe cases hydrocephalus and brainstem/optic-chiasm atrophy) and to a seizure-prone cortical network in a subset of patients.

Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36087421 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Microcephaly, developmental delay, hypotonia, corpus callosum abnormalities, deafness, and retinal atrophy were common features in the previously described cases."
Literature review documents the recurrent structural brain anomalies (microcephaly, corpus callosum abnormalities) that this node accounts for.
Cardiac developmental transcriptional dysregulation
In a subset of affected individuals, MED13/Mediator dysfunction perturbs cardiac gene-expression programs, contributing to mild and variable congenital heart anomalies. Mutations in the Mediator complex, including MED13 and its paralog MED13L, have been linked to both congenital heart defects and cardiovascular diseases. In the adult heart, MED13 (also known as THRAP1/TRAP240) participates in nuclear-receptor signaling that drives the transcription of genes regulating cardiac and systemic energy homeostasis — this adult-cardiac metabolic role is the best-evidenced mechanism in current literature, with the connection to congenital cardiac developmental programs established by clinical observation and corroborated by mouse genetics demonstrating functional redundancy between Med13 and Med13L in cardiac transcription.

Congenital heart defects Perturbed cardiac transcriptional programs contribute to the variable congenital heart phenotype reported in a subset of MED13 cases.

PMID:40989238 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Previous studies have linked mutations in the Mediator complex, specifically Mediator 13 (Med13) and Mediator 13-like (Med13L), with both congenital heart defects and cardiovascular diseases."
Mouse cardiac KO study acknowledges the established link between MED13/MED13L mutations and congenital heart defects, supporting the cardiac developmental arm of MED13 syndrome pathophysiology.
PMID:33390853 SUPPORT Other
"cardiac MED13 mainly participates in the regulation of nuclear receptor signaling, which drives the transcription of genes involved in modulating cardiac and systemic energy homeostasis"
Review describes MED13's nuclear-receptor-coupled transcriptional role in the heart. This evidence documents the cardiac metabolic/homeostatic mechanism; the extension to congenital heart development is an inference supported by the clinical phenotype.
PMID:25422356 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We previously showed that cardiac MED13 modulates systemic energy homeostasis in mice."
Mouse study demonstrates MED13's role in cardiac metabolic gene regulation. This adult-cardiac metabolic evidence complements the developmental arm; direct developmental cardiac gene-expression evidence is still lacking.
Craniofacial, ocular, and sensory developmental dysregulation
Because the Mediator complex is required for transcription of essentially all RNA polymerase II genes, MED13 dysfunction perturbs developmental gene-expression programs in neural-crest- and cranial-placode-derived tissues. This contributes to facial dysmorphism, ocular motility anomalies (Duane anomaly) and optic-nerve abnormalities, sensorineural hearing loss, dental anomalies, and, rarely, enteric nervous system involvement (congenital aganglionic megacolon).

Eye and vision abnormalities Disrupted ocular and visual-pathway development underlies the grouped eye/vision phenotype, the most consistent non-neurological finding in the cohort.

Somatic growth and skeletal developmental dysregulation
Broad transcriptional pleiotropy of MED13/Mediator dysfunction extends to somatic growth and skeletal/limb developmental programs, contributing to restricted growth and skeletal and limb abnormalities in a subset of patients.

PMID:41195223 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The main symptoms are intellectual disability (ID) of varying degrees, developmental delay (DD), hypotonia during infancy, facial dysmorphism, language impairment, restricted growth, skeletal and limb abnormalities, and behavioral abnormalities."
Restricted growth and skeletal/limb abnormalities are listed among the main symptoms of MRD61, supporting this developmental arm.
MED13/MED13L paralog redundancy
MED13 and MED13L are mutually exclusive occupants of the same position in the CKM and are partially redundant, so the intact paralog buffers loss of the other in a tissue-dependent way. Combined cardiomyocyte deletion of both is lethal within weeks where either single deletion is tolerated, and in the early mouse embryo Med13l compensates for Med13 loss during oocyte growth but cannot rescue post-implantation development. This buffering is the most plausible explanation for why MED13 haploinsufficiency produces a predominantly neurodevelopmental phenotype with only mild, variable cardiac involvement, and it is the shared axis linking this entry to MED13L syndrome.

PMID:40989238 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Med13 and Med13L are mutually exclusive paralogs within the kinase submodule of the Mediator complex that have been shown to have partially redundant functions in embryonic development and transcription, but their combined roles have not been investigated in the adult heart."
States the mutual exclusivity and partial redundancy underlying this node.
PMID:40989238 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Med13 and Med13L function partially redundantly within the heart to maintain basal cardiac function and transcription, as well as redundancies within cardiac phenotypes related to mediator complex disruptions."
Establishes tissue-level redundancy in the heart, the organ where MED13 syndrome's phenotype is mildest.
PMID:29325037 SUPPORT Model Organism
"MED13 is essential for ZGA in the mouse, in part by regulating expression of the embryo-specific chromatin remodeling complex, esBAF."
Shows a developmental window in which MED13 is required and paralog compensation is insufficient.
Stress-induced Med13 degradation and cyclin C release
A conserved, non-transcriptional arm characterised in yeast: under oxidative or nutrient stress, Snf1/AMPK together with the cell-wall-integrity MAPK pathway drive degradation of Med13, and a Snx4/Ksp1-dependent selective-autophagy route provides a second, proteasome-independent clearance path. Destroying Med13 releases cyclin C from its Med13-dependent nuclear anchor to relocalise to mitochondria, where it promotes fission and sensitises cells to regulated cell death. If a mammalian counterpart exists in neurons, MED13-haploinsufficient cells would be predicted to sit closer to the cyclin-C release threshold. The same axis is directly demonstrated in human MED13L patient fibroblasts, which is the strongest argument that it is not purely a yeast phenomenon — but it has not been shown for MED13 in human cells.

Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy with mitochondrial dysfunction Cytoplasmic cyclin C drives mitochondrial fission, the candidate mechanism for the autopsy-confirmed mitochondrial abnormalities and reduced respiratory chain complex activity in the one reported MED13 patient with cardiomyopathy and hepatomegaly. Marked as a candidate link: the yeast mechanism and the human observation have not been connected experimentally.

MED13 c.C979T (p.Pro327Ser) Pathogenic
Gene: MED13 hgnc:22474 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in MED13 (hgnc:22474). hgnc:22474 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. missense

17q23.2 800 kb deletion encompassing MED13 Pathogenic
Gene: MED13 hgnc:22474 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in MED13 (hgnc:22474). hgnc:22474 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. deletion
De novo whole-gene deletion detected by array CGH in a child with moderate intellectual disability, short stature, mild dysmorphism and hearing loss. The authors attributed the cataract, hearing loss and semicircular canal dysplasia specifically to MED13 haploinsufficiency, making this the independent CNV support for a dosage mechanism. Caveat: the deletion spans six genes and the patient also carried a supernumerary marker chromosome 17, so attribution to MED13 alone is the authors' inference rather than a demonstrated fact. Cataract and semicircular canal dysplasia are not curated as MED13 syndrome phenotypes on that basis.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:22162340 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Unexpectedly, aCGH analysis also revealed a de novo deletion of 800 kb encompassing six genes in the 17q23.2 region, including MED13 (also known as THRAP1)."
Documents the whole-gene deletion.
PMID:22162340 PARTIAL Human Clinical
"We cannot entirely exclude a minor role for the SMC(17), but we suggest that MED13 haploinsufficiency was responsible for the phenotype of the patient particularly the cataract, hearing loss and semicircular canal dysplasia."
Attributes specific features to MED13 dosage loss while explicitly acknowledging the confounding supernumerary marker chromosome — support is therefore PARTIAL.

External Assertions

1
ClinGen gene-disease validity - MED13 and complex neurodevelopmental disorder
ClinGen gene disease validity CGGV:assertion_e82f63b1-ed0f-4dd0-8168-3aa4e0a4c2a9-2022-05-17T100000.000Z
The ClinGen Intellectual Disability and Autism Gene Curation Expert Panel classified the MED13 to complex neurodevelopmental disorder relationship as Definitive under SOP9 on 2022-05-17, with autosomal dominant inheritance and loss of function as the stated mechanism. This is the authoritative gene-disease validity anchor for the entry, and the Definitive level is what licenses full-strength PVS1 for truncating alleles.

Early speech and language therapy is recommended to address the prominent communication delays that are among the most consistent features of MED13 syndrome.

Genetic counseling is recommended for affected families. Most cases are de novo but parent-to-child transmission has been documented, necessitating parental testing and recurrence risk assessment.

Antiseizure medication is used to manage seizures in the subset of MED13 patients with epilepsy, including those presenting with developmental and epileptic encephalopathy or myoclonic-atonic seizures.
🔬

Diagnosis

3
Clinical whole-exome or genome sequencing
Every reported MED13 diagnosis has been made by exome or genome sequencing. There is no MED13-specific clinical gestalt reliable enough to prompt single-gene testing — one proband carried a clinical diagnosis of Kabuki syndrome — so sequencing is the only practical route. Trio testing establishes de novo status, which carries substantial pathogenicity weight for this gene.
clinical whole-exome sequencing NCIT:C101295 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Markers: MED13
Results: Heterozygous pathogenic or likely pathogenic MED13 variant
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34211152 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We strongly recommend that ES/GS be considered as a first- or second-tier test for patients with CA/DD/ID."
Places exome/genome sequencing in the recommended pathway for the presentation MED13 patients arrive with.
PMID:41561257 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"underscoring the importance of exome sequencing in patients with neurodevelopmental disorders and congenital anomalies"
Case-level confirmation of the diagnostic route.
Chromosomal microarray analysis
Chromosomal microarray remains first-tier for unexplained developmental delay and detects the 17q23.2 deletions encompassing MED13 that account for a small subset of cases. It is normal in the great majority of MED13 patients, so a normal microarray does not exclude the diagnosis and should prompt sequencing.
chromosomal microarray testing NCIT:C18477 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Markers: 17q23.2
Results: Normal in most; detects 17q23.2 deletions encompassing MED13 in the CNV subset
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20466091 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Available evidence strongly supports the use of CMA in place of G-banded karyotyping as the first-tier cytogenetic diagnostic test for patients with DD/ID, ASD, or MCA."
Establishes microarray's first-tier position in the diagnostic pathway.
PMID:22162340 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Array comparative genome hybridization (aCGH) was used to determine the gene content and proximal and distal breakpoints of the small supernumerary marker chromosome (SMC)."
Demonstrates the array-based route by which a MED13 deletion is found.
ACMG/AMP variant interpretation
Reported de novo MED13 truncating alleles have been classified pathogenic under the ACMG/AMP framework by combining PVS1 (null variant in a gene where loss of function is the disease mechanism) with PS2 (confirmed de novo). Because ClinGen's PVS1 refinement conditions the strength of that criterion on gene-disease validity, MED13's Definitive ClinGen classification is what licenses full-strength PVS1 here.
molecular genetic testing NCIT:C19770 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Results: PVS1 + PS2 for de novo truncating alleles; missense alleles largely remain of uncertain significance absent functional data
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:25741868 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This report recommends the use of specific standard terminology-"pathogenic," "likely pathogenic," "uncertain significance," "likely benign," and "benign"-to describe variants identified in genes that cause Mendelian disorders."
The classification framework applied to MED13 variants.
PMID:30192042 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Furthermore, this guideline did not take into account the relative strengths for each evidence type and the final outcome of their combinations with respect to PVS1 strength."
The ClinGen SVI refinement that makes PVS1 strength contingent on gene-disease validity, which is why the Definitive ClinGen classification for MED13 matters practically.

Prevalence

1
Worldwide
Cases In Literature <1 in 1,000,000
Approximately 26 cases have been reported worldwide as of 2025, placing MED13 syndrome firmly in the ultra-rare category. No population-based prevalence estimate is yet available.

"To date, only 26 cases of MRD61 have been reported worldwide."
Confirms the ultra-rare status of MED13 syndrome with fewer than 30 cases described globally as of 2025.

Differential Diagnoses

6

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from MED13 Syndrome:

MED13L syndrome MONDO:0014773
Overlapping Features The paralogous CKM-hinge disorder and the closest differential. MED13 and MED13L occupy the same structural position in the kinase module and produce overlapping intellectual disability with prominent speech impairment.
Distinguishing Features
  • MED13L has a recognisable facial gestalt (bulbous nasal tip, depressed nasal bridge, hypotonic open mouth) and roughly four times as many reported cases.
  • MED13 carries ophthalmologic features not reported in MED13L, notably Duane anomaly and retinal atrophy.
  • Resolved definitively by which gene carries the variant; the two are not clinically separable with confidence.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29740699 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Mutations in several other genes encoding subunits of Mediator have been previously shown to associate with DD/ID, including MED13L, a paralog of MED13."
Establishes MED13L as the paralogous disease gene, the basis for the differential-diagnosis relationship.
MED12-related intellectual disability syndrome Not Yet Curated MONDO:0100000
Overlapping Features The X-linked member of the CDK8 kinase module allelic series (FG/Opitz-Kaveggia, Lujan-Fryns, Ohdo-Maat-Kievit-Brunner), overlapping MED13 syndrome in intellectual disability and hypotonia.
Distinguishing Features
  • X-linked inheritance with predominantly affected males, versus autosomal dominant de novo events in MED13.
  • MED12 activates CDK8 kinase activity whereas MED13 anchors the module, so the molecular lesions are non-equivalent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:30905399 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Mutations in MED12, MED13, and MED13L were previously identified in syndromic developmental disorders with overlapping phenotypes."
States the phenotypic overlap across the kinase-module disorders that makes them mutual differentials.
CDK8-related syndromic developmental disorder MONDO:0032897
Overlapping Features De novo missense substitutions in CDK8, the kinase subunit of the same module, produce hypotonia, mild-to-moderate intellectual disability, behavioural disorders and variable facial dysmorphism, with congenital heart disease, corpus callosum agenesis, seizures and sensory impairments in subsets — a phenotype the authors themselves describe as similar to the other kinase-module disorders.
Distinguishing Features
  • CDK8 variants localise to the ATP-binding pocket of the kinase domain and act by reducing kinase activity; MED13 loss is an anchoring defect with intact kinase function.
  • Ano-rectal malformations are reported in multiple CDK8 individuals and are not a MED13 feature.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:30905399 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Affected individuals have overlapping phenotypes characterized by hypotonia, mild to moderate intellectual disability, behavioral disorders, and variable facial dysmorphism."
Defines the overlapping CDK8 phenotype.
PMID:30905399 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We conclude that missense mutations in CDK8 cause a developmental disorder that has phenotypic similarity to syndromes associated with mutations in other subunits of the Mediator kinase module, indicating probable overlap in pathogenic mechanisms."
The authors' own statement that the kinase-module disorders overlap phenotypically and mechanistically.
CDK19-related developmental and epileptic encephalopathy MONDO:0030059
Overlapping Features De novo CDK19 variants (the CDK8 paralog in the same module) produce hypotonia, global developmental delay, epileptic encephalopathy and dysmorphic features — the closest match to the severe epileptic tail of the MED13 phenotype.
Distinguishing Features
  • Epileptic encephalopathy is a core CDK19 feature, whereas in MED13 it is a rare severe-tail presentation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32330417 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These individuals presented with hypotonia, global developmental delay, epileptic encephalopathy, and dysmorphic features."
Defines the CDK19 phenotype overlapping the MED13 severe tail.
MED12L-related intellectual disability (Nizon-Isidor syndrome) MONDO:0030030
Overlapping Features Haploinsufficiency of MED12L, the fourth kinase-module paralog gene, causes intellectual disability and/or developmental delay with speech impairment, autism spectrum disorder and corpus callosum abnormality — overlapping MED13 syndrome closely.
Distinguishing Features
  • Corpus callosum abnormality is a prominent and recurrent MED12L feature.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:31155615 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"All affected individuals presented with intellectual disability and/or developmental delay, including speech impairment. Other features included autism spectrum disorder, aggressive behavior, corpus callosum abnormality, and mild facial morphological features."
Defines the overlapping MED12L phenotype.
PMID:31155615 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Other subunits of the kinase module have been already implicated in intellectual disability, namely MED12, MED13L, MED13, and CDK19."
Names MED13 explicitly among the kinase-module intellectual disability genes, establishing the family of mutual differentials.
Kabuki syndrome MONDO:0016512
Overlapping Features A MED13 proband carried a clinical diagnosis of Kabuki syndrome with negative KMT2D and KDM6A testing before exome sequencing found a de novo MED13 p.Pro327Ser variant. MED13 therefore belongs in the differential for sequencing-negative Kabuki-like presentations.
Distinguishing Features
  • Kabuki syndrome is resolved by identifying a KMT2D or KDM6A variant; MED13 should be considered when those are negative.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33258286 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report the case of a 13-year-old girl who received a previous clinical diagnosis of Kabuki syndrome (KS) without mutations in classic KS genes."
Documents the Kabuki-like presentation resolved as MED13, the basis for this differential.
PMID:33258286 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The aim of this report is to improve clinical delineation of MED13-related condition and to explore differences and similarities between KS spectrum and MED13-related disorders."
Frames the two conditions as an explicit differential-diagnosis pair.

Related Datasets

2
Cardiomyocyte-specific Deletion of Med13 and Med13L Results in Dysregulated Gene Expression and Lethal Heart Failure geo:GSE298801
Bulk RNA-seq from adult murine cardiomyocytes after inducible knockout of Med13 and Med13L. Double knockout results in lethal heart failure within 6 weeks, with significant gene dysregulation of fibrotic pathways and calcium handling. Demonstrates that Med13 and Med13L function redundantly in the adult heart to maintain basal cardiac function and transcription, relevant to the congenital heart defects observed in MED13 syndrome.
mouse BULK RNA SEQ n=8

Findings
Med13 and Med13L are functionally redundant in adult cardiomyocytes

Mouse model demonstrates functional redundancy of Med13 and Med13L in heart and lethal consequences of combined loss.

Collects medical history, developmental milestones, behavioral assessments, and longitudinal follow-up data, providing natural-history data for MED13 syndrome.

Animal Models

3
In utero shRNA knockdown of Med13 in embryonic neocortex Mus musculus
In utero electroporation knockdown of Med13 in developing mouse cortical neurons impairs radial migration, contralateral (callosal) axonal projection and dendritic complexity. Proteomics in MED13-deleted human neuroblastoma cells identified PLXNA4 as a dysregulated downstream effector, and PlxnA4 overexpression rescues the migration and callosal projection defects but not the dendritic phenotype — separating the pathway into a PlxnA4-dependent and a PlxnA4-independent arm.
Impaired radial neuronal migration Impaired callosal projection Reduced dendritic complexity

Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41663567 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We found that silencing Med13 in cortical neurons impaired its radial migration and contralateral projection as well as dendritic complexity in mice."
Establishes the cortical neuronal phenotype of Med13 knockdown.
PMID:41663567 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Notably, the impaired radial migration and callosal projection, but not dendritic complexity, were largely restored by overexpression of PlxnA4 in Med13 knock-down neurons."
Identifies PlxnA4 as the effector for the migration/projection arm and shows the dendritic arm is mechanistically separate.
Inducible cardiomyocyte-specific Med13 and Med13l double knockout Mus musculus
Tamoxifen-inducible combined deletion of Med13 and Med13l in adult cardiomyocytes causes lethal heart failure with a median survival of six weeks, with dysregulation of fibrotic and calcium-handling pathways shared across Mediator cardiac knockouts. Single deletion of either paralog is tolerated, which is the direct demonstration of paralog redundancy.

Cardiac-specific Med13 transgenic overexpression and cardiomyocyte deletion (miR-208a axis) Mus musculus
Gain- and loss-of-function models of cardiac MED13 dosage. Overexpression, or pharmacologic inhibition of the MED13-targeting microRNA miR-208a, confers resistance to diet-induced obesity; cardiomyocyte-specific deletion enhances obesity and worsens metabolic syndrome. This defines the systemic metabolic arm of MED13 biology.

Source YAML

click to show 
name: MED13 Syndrome
creation_date: '2026-04-11T12:00:00Z'
category: Mendelian
synonyms:
- Intellectual developmental disorder, autosomal dominant 61
- MRD61
- MED13-related intellectual disability
- MED13-related syndrome
- MED13-related disorder
description: >
  MED13 syndrome (MRD61) is a rare autosomal dominant neurodevelopmental disorder
  caused by heterozygous pathogenic variants in MED13, which encodes mediator
  complex subunit 13. The Mediator complex is a multi-subunit transcriptional
  coactivator that bridges gene-specific transcription factors to RNA polymerase II.
  MED13 is part of the CDK8 kinase module, which reversibly associates with the
  Mediator core. Pathogenic MED13 variants disrupt transcriptional regulation in
  developing tissues, leading to intellectual disability, speech and language delay,
  variable congenital heart defects, facial dysmorphism, hypotonia, and behavioral
  abnormalities including autism spectrum disorder and ADHD. To date, only
  approximately 26 cases have been reported worldwide. Both truncating
  (haploinsufficiency) and clustered missense variants (affecting a conserved
  phosphodegron at Thr326/Pro327) have been identified. The phenotypic spectrum
  continues to expand with subsequent case reports, which have added structural
  brain anomalies (hydrocephalus, optic chiasm and brainstem atrophy), skeletal
  and limb abnormalities, restricted growth, dental anomalies (supernumerary
  teeth), and rare enteric involvement (congenital aganglionic megacolon).

mechanistic_hypotheses:
- hypothesis_group_id: med13_haploinsufficiency
  hypothesis_label: MED13 haploinsufficiency as the mechanism for truncating alleles
  status: CANONICAL
  description: >
    Heterozygous truncating MED13 variants and whole-gene 17q23.2 deletions reduce the
    pool of CDK8 kinase module available to dock on core Mediator. Supported by the
    predominance of de novo truncating alleles in the founding cohort, by an independent
    whole-gene deletion phenocopy, and by the explicit haploinsufficiency conclusion
    drawn from a functionally characterised frameshift case.
  applies_to_subtypes:

    explanation: >-
      Independent whole-gene dosage-loss support: a 17q23.2 microdeletion encompassing
      MED13 reproduces a MED13-attributable phenotype.
- hypothesis_group_id: med13_missense_altered_function
  hypothesis_label: Clustered missense variants act by altered function rather than simple dosage loss
  status: EMERGING
  description: >
    Pathogenic MED13 missense variants cluster in two small regions — an N-terminal
    region containing the Thr326/Pro327 SCF-Fbw7 phosphodegron and a C-terminal region —
    plus a central pair of residues around 834/835. The clustering itself argues against
    simple loss of function, which would tolerate variants anywhere. Clinically, the most
    severe reported presentations (developmental and epileptic encephalopathy, neonatal
    multi-organ disease with mitochondrial pathology) have so far occurred with missense
    rather than truncating alleles. Degron-region variants are predicted to stabilise
    MED13 against Fbw7-mediated turnover, which would raise rather than lower CKM
    occupancy. This is structured inference from position and phenotype; no functional
    assay has yet demonstrated altered function for any MED13 missense allele.
  applies_to_subtypes:
  - Missense (N-terminal degron, central 834/835, C-terminal clusters)

    reference_title: "ClinGen Gene-Disease Validity: MED13 - complex neurodevelopmental disorder"
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "The mechanism of pathogenicity is loss of function."
    explanation: >-
      The ClinGen Intellectual Disability and Autism Gene Curation Expert Panel, curating
      across nonsense, frameshift, missense and in-frame indel alleles, concluded the
      mechanism is loss of function — the authoritative counterweight to an
      altered-function reading of the missense cluster.
  notes: >-
    Kept as EMERGING rather than CANONICAL, and explicitly contested. The clustering and
    severity signals are real, but no functional data distinguish altered function from a
    severe hypomorph, ClinGen's expert-panel curation across the full allelic series
    concluded loss of function, and the same p.Pro835Ser allele has been reported with
    quite different clinical courses.
- hypothesis_group_id: med13_fbw7_degron_stabilisation
  hypothesis_label: Fbw7-degron stabilisation as a dosage-restoration strategy
  status: EMERGING
  description: >
    If SCF-Fbw7-mediated turnover sets the steady-state level of MED13 protein, then
    slowing turnover of the product of the intact allele in a haploinsufficient cell
    could raise MED13 toward the diploid level and restore CKM occupancy. This is the
    one route from MED13 mechanism to a candidate therapeutic handle. The major caveat
    is target selectivity: FBXW7 is a broadly acting tumour suppressor, so
    non-selective inhibition of its ligase activity carries oncogenic risk and any
    viable strategy would need to spare its other substrates.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:23322298
    reference_title: "The SCF-Fbw7 ubiquitin ligase degrades MED13 and MED13L and regulates CDK8 module association with Mediator."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      MED13/13L physically link the CDK8 module to Mediator, and Fbw7 loss increases CDK8
      module-Mediator association.
    explanation: >-
      Demonstrates that reducing Fbw7 activity raises CKM-Mediator association, the
      effect the strategy would exploit.
  - reference: PMID:17909001
    reference_title: "FBXW7/hCDC4 is a general tumor suppressor in human cancer."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Our results indicate that FBXW7 is inactivated by mutation in diverse human cancer
      types with an overall mutation frequency of approximately 6%.
    explanation: >-
      Counter-evidence establishing the oncogenic risk of reducing FBXW7 function, the
      principal safety objection to this strategy.
- hypothesis_group_id: med13_mir208a_metabolic_axis
  hypothesis_label: The cardiac miR-208a/MED13 metabolic axis as a systemic-phenotype modifier
  status: EMERGING
  description: >
    In mice, cardiac MED13 acts through nuclear-receptor signalling to control systemic
    energy expenditure: cardiac MED13 overexpression or miR-208a inhibition confers
    resistance to diet-induced obesity, while cardiomyocyte MED13 deletion worsens it.
    If this axis is dosage-sensitive in humans, it offers a candidate explanation for the
    restricted growth reported in a subset of MRD61 patients that is independent of the
    neurodevelopmental phenotype, and predicts that variant class might track with growth
    and cardiac trajectory. No human data test this.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22541436
    reference_title: "A cardiac microRNA governs systemic energy homeostasis by regulation of MED13."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Cardiac-specific overexpression of MED13 or pharmacologic inhibition of miR-208a in
      mice confers resistance to high-fat diet-induced obesity and improves systemic
      insulin sensitivity and glucose tolerance.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the bidirectional, dosage-dependent link from cardiac MED13 level to
      systemic metabolism.
  - reference: PMID:22541436
    reference_title: "A cardiac microRNA governs systemic energy homeostasis by regulation of MED13."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Conversely, genetic deletion of MED13 specifically in cardiomyocytes enhances
      obesity in response to high-fat diet and exacerbates metabolic syndrome.
    explanation: >-
      The loss-of-function direction, which is the one relevant to MED13
      haploinsufficiency in patients.
  notes: >-
    Entirely murine. The human MRD61 growth phenotype is restricted growth rather than
    obesity, so the direction of any human effect is not established; this is a
    hypothesis about a candidate modifier axis, not a claimed mechanism.
pathophysiology:
- name: CKM hinge anchoring by MED13
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >
    MED13 is the hinge subunit that physically tethers the four-protein CDK8 kinase
    module (CKM: MED13, MED12, CDK8, cyclin C) to core Mediator. Structural work shows
    MED13 adopts an Argonaute-like bi-lobal fold, and that a large intrinsically
    disordered region within it forms a principal CKM-core interface which directly
    occludes the RNA polymerase II and MED26 binding sites — making CKM docking a
    kinase-independent repressor of Mediator-dependent transcription. Division of labour
    within the module is asymmetric: MED12 activates CDK8 kinase activity, whereas MED13
    mediates the association of the module with Mediator. This node is what MED13 dosage
    and structural integrity actually control, and is the mechanistic layer the previous
    version of this entry summarised only as "connective role".
  genes:
  - preferred_term: MED13
    term:
      id: hgnc:22474
      label: MED13
  - preferred_term: MED12
    term:
      id: hgnc:11957
      label: MED12
  - preferred_term: CDK8
    term:
      id: hgnc:1779
      label: CDK8
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
    term:

  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: transcription coregulator activity
    term:
      id: GO:0003712
      label: transcription coregulator activity

  - target: Stress-induced Med13 degradation and cyclin C release
    description: >-
      Because MED13 is the nuclear anchor for cyclin C as well as the CKM hinge, a

      releases cyclin C to the cytoplasm.
- name: CKM occupancy of core Mediator and bidirectional transcriptional control
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >
    CKM docking on core Mediator sterically blocks RNA polymerase II recruitment, and
    this repression is independent of CDK8 catalytic activity — MED12 and MED13, not
    kinase activity, are the critical subunits for it. The module is nevertheless not a
    pure repressor: CDK8 also acts as a context-dependent co-activator across the p53,
    Wnt/beta-catenin, serum-response, SMAD and thyroid-hormone-receptor programs.
    Consequently a perturbation of MED13 dosage dysregulates transcription
    bidirectionally at CKM-gated promoters and enhancers rather than simply de-repressing
    them, which is why MED13-related disease is a transcriptional dysregulation disorder
    and not a straightforward loss of repression.

- name: Disrupted Mediator complex transcriptional regulation
  description: >
    MED13 is a subunit of the CDK8 kinase module of the Mediator complex, which
    regulates RNA polymerase II-dependent transcription. MED13 plays a connective
    role between the CDK8 kinase module and the Mediator core. Pathogenic variants
    disrupt Mediator-dependent transcriptional control and broader gene-expression
    programs. MED13 belongs to the group of CDK8-kinase module-associated disease
    genes, alongside MED12, MED13L, and CDK8.

- name: MED13 phosphodegron disruption and impaired protein turnover
  description: >
    Several pathogenic missense variants cluster at a conserved phosphodegron
    (Thr326/Pro327) in MED13. This phosphodegron is normally recognized by the
    SCF-Fbw7 ubiquitin ligase complex, leading to MED13 ubiquitination and
    proteasomal degradation. Mutations at these residues may impair MED13 protein
    turnover, potentially resulting in gain-of-function or dominant-negative
    effects through accumulation of mutant protein.

- name: Neurodevelopmental transcriptional dysregulation
  description: >
    Downstream of MED13/Mediator dysfunction, neuronal gene-expression programs
    are dysregulated during brain development, contributing to intellectual
    disability, global developmental delay, language impairment, and variable
    behavioral phenotypes. Mechanistic studies in mice indicate that MED13 is
    required cell-autonomously for cortical neuron development: knockdown of Med13
    in cortical neurons impairs radial migration, contralateral axonal projection,
    and dendritic complexity, in part through PlxnA4, linking MED13 dysfunction to
    the neurodevelopmental disorder phenotype.

- name: Impaired brain structural development and network excitability
  description: >

    (microcephaly, corpus callosum abnormalities, and in severe cases
    hydrocephalus and brainstem/optic-chiasm atrophy) and to a seizure-prone
    cortical network in a subset of patients.

- name: Cardiac developmental transcriptional dysregulation
  description: >
    In a subset of affected individuals, MED13/Mediator dysfunction perturbs
    cardiac gene-expression programs, contributing to mild and variable congenital
    heart anomalies. Mutations in the Mediator complex, including MED13 and its
    paralog MED13L, have been linked to both congenital heart defects and
    cardiovascular diseases. In the adult heart, MED13 (also known as THRAP1/TRAP240)
    participates in nuclear-receptor signaling that drives the transcription of genes
    regulating cardiac and systemic energy homeostasis — this adult-cardiac metabolic
    role is the best-evidenced mechanism in current literature, with the connection to
    congenital cardiac developmental programs established by clinical observation and
    corroborated by mouse genetics demonstrating functional redundancy between Med13
    and Med13L in cardiac transcription.

    description: >-
      Perturbed cardiac transcriptional programs contribute to the variable
      congenital heart phenotype reported in a subset of MED13 cases.
- name: Craniofacial, ocular, and sensory developmental dysregulation
  description: >
    Because the Mediator complex is required for transcription of essentially all
    RNA polymerase II genes, MED13 dysfunction perturbs developmental gene-expression
    programs in neural-crest- and cranial-placode-derived tissues. This contributes
    to facial dysmorphism, ocular motility anomalies (Duane anomaly) and optic-nerve
    abnormalities, sensorineural hearing loss, dental anomalies, and, rarely, enteric
    nervous system involvement (congenital aganglionic megacolon).
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: neural crest cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000333
      label: migratory neural crest cell
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: neural crest cell differentiation
    term:
      id: GO:0014033
      label: neural crest cell differentiation
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
  - preferred_term: eye development
    term:
      id: GO:0001654
      label: eye development
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
  evidence:

    reference_title: "MED13 mutation: A novel cause of developmental and epileptic encephalopathy with infantile spasms."
    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-
      The reported MED13 phenotype spectrum includes ocular abnormalities and
      facial dysmorphism, supporting a craniofacial/sensory developmental arm.
  downstream:

  - target: Aganglionic megacolon
    description: >-

      aganglionic megacolon in a single reported case.
  - target: Chronic constipation
    description: >-
      Enteric/autonomic dysmotility (a milder counterpart of the rare aganglionic
      megacolon) contributes to chronic constipation, a common gastrointestinal
      manifestation in the original cohort.
  - target: Eye and vision abnormalities
    description: >-
      Disrupted ocular and visual-pathway development underlies the grouped eye/vision
      phenotype, the most consistent non-neurological finding in the cohort.

- name: MED13/MED13L paralog redundancy
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >
    MED13 and MED13L are mutually exclusive occupants of the same position in the CKM
    and are partially redundant, so the intact paralog buffers loss of the other in a
    tissue-dependent way. Combined cardiomyocyte deletion of both is lethal within weeks
    where either single deletion is tolerated, and in the early mouse embryo Med13l
    compensates for Med13 loss during oocyte growth but cannot rescue post-implantation
    development. This buffering is the most plausible explanation for why MED13
    haploinsufficiency produces a predominantly neurodevelopmental phenotype with only
    mild, variable cardiac involvement, and it is the shared axis linking this entry to
    MED13L syndrome.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: MED13
    term:
      id: hgnc:22474
      label: MED13
  - preferred_term: MED13L
    term:
      id: hgnc:22962
      label: MED13L
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
    term:
      id: GO:0006357
      label: regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40989238
    reference_title: "Med13 and Med13L: Critical redundant players in basal cardiac function and gene expression."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Med13 and Med13L are mutually exclusive paralogs within the kinase submodule of the
      Mediator complex that have been shown to have partially redundant functions in
      embryonic development and transcription, but their combined roles have not been
      investigated in the adult heart.
    explanation: States the mutual exclusivity and partial redundancy underlying this node.
  - reference: PMID:40989238
    reference_title: "Med13 and Med13L: Critical redundant players in basal cardiac function and gene expression."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Med13 and Med13L function partially redundantly within the heart to maintain basal
      cardiac function and transcription, as well as redundancies within cardiac
      phenotypes related to mediator complex disruptions.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes tissue-level redundancy in the heart, the organ where MED13 syndrome's
      phenotype is mildest.
  - reference: PMID:29325037
    reference_title: "Mediator complex component MED13 regulates zygotic genome activation and is required for postimplantation development in the mouse."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      MED13 is essential for ZGA in the mouse, in part by regulating expression of the
      embryo-specific chromatin remodeling complex, esBAF.
    explanation: >-
      Shows a developmental window in which MED13 is required and paralog compensation is
      insufficient.
  downstream:
  - target: CKM occupancy of core Mediator and bidirectional transcriptional control
    description: >-
      The degree of paralog compensation in a given tissue determines how much functional
      CKM survives MED13 dosage loss there.
- name: Stress-induced Med13 degradation and cyclin C release
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >
    A conserved, non-transcriptional arm characterised in yeast: under oxidative or
    nutrient stress, Snf1/AMPK together with the cell-wall-integrity MAPK pathway drive
    degradation of Med13, and a Snx4/Ksp1-dependent selective-autophagy route provides a
    second, proteasome-independent clearance path. Destroying Med13 releases cyclin C
    from its Med13-dependent nuclear anchor to relocalise to mitochondria, where it
    promotes fission and sensitises cells to regulated cell death. If a mammalian
    counterpart exists in neurons, MED13-haploinsufficient cells would be predicted to
    sit closer to the cyclin-C release threshold. The same axis is directly demonstrated
    in human MED13L patient fibroblasts, which is the strongest argument that it is not
    purely a yeast phenomenon — but it has not been shown for MED13 in human cells.
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: neuron
    term:
      id: CL:0000540
      label: neuron
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: macroautophagy
    term:
      id: GO:0016236
      label: macroautophagy
  - preferred_term: response to oxidative stress
    term:
      id: GO:0006979
      label: response to oxidative stress
  - preferred_term: mitochondrial fission
    term:
      id: GO:0000266
      label: mitochondrial fission
    modifier: INCREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30175106
    reference_title: "Snf1 cooperates with the CWI MAPK pathway to mediate the degradation of Med13 following oxidative stress."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      In Saccharomyces cerevisiae, oxidative stress triggers Med13 destruction, which
      releases cyclin C into the cytoplasm to promote mitochondrial fission and programmed
      cell death.
    explanation: >-
      States the stress-induced Med13-degradation-to-cyclin-C-release axis in the
      organism where it was characterised.
  - reference: PMID:37733395
    reference_title: "Ksp1 is an autophagic receptor protein for the Snx4-assisted autophagy of Ssn2/Med13."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Here, we describe a kinase-independent role of Ksp1 as a novel autophagic receptor
      protein for Ssn2/Med13, a known cargo of Snx4-assisted autophagy of transcription
      factors.
    explanation: >-
      Documents the selective-autophagy clearance route for Med13, a proteasome-independent
      lever on Med13 protein level.
  - reference: PMID:30621145
    reference_title: "Cyclin C: The Story of a Non-Cycling Cyclin."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Upon oxidative stress, cyclin C leaves the nucleus and directly activates the
      guanosine 5'-triphosphatase (GTPase) Drp1, or Dnm1 in yeast, to induce mitochondrial
      fragmentation.
    explanation: >-
      Identifies the effector step (Drp1 activation) by which released cyclin C fragments
      mitochondria, and notes the mammalian Drp1 counterpart.
  downstream:
  - target: Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy with mitochondrial dysfunction
    description: >-
      Cytoplasmic cyclin C drives mitochondrial fission, the candidate mechanism for the
      autopsy-confirmed mitochondrial abnormalities and reduced respiratory chain complex
      activity in the one reported MED13 patient with cardiomyopathy and hepatomegaly.
      Marked as a candidate link: the yeast mechanism and the human observation have not
      been connected experimentally.
  notes: >-
    Retained deliberately as a low-confidence node. The mechanism is established in yeast
    and its downstream consequence is demonstrated in human MED13L-mutant fibroblasts
    (PMID:35198885, curated on the MED13L_Syndrome entry), but no study has shown
    stress-induced MED13 degradation or cyclin C release in MED13-haploinsufficient human
    cells. The single downstream edge is therefore a candidate explanation for the
    mitochondrial phenotype, not a demonstrated causal chain.

- category: Clinical
  name: Autism spectrum disorder
  frequency: OCCASIONAL
  description: >
    Autism spectrum disorder has been reported in a subset of individuals with
    MED13-related disorder. The syndrome-defining cohort supports ASD as a
    recurrent but non-universal feature, and a subsequent case report of a
    patient with a pathogenic de novo truncating MED13 variant further supports
    a genuine MED13-ASD association. A separate 2024 single-case report described
    ASD in a child with an uncertain heterozygous MED13 variant and should be
    interpreted cautiously.
  notes: >-
    PMID:38854223 is hypothesis-generating only: the reported MED13 variant was
    of uncertain significance, so that case should not be treated as standalone
    proof of a pathogenic MED13-ASD association. PMID:38528425, by contrast,
    reports ASD in a patient with a pathogenic de novo frameshift (truncating)
    MED13 variant, providing firmer evidence for the association.

- category: Clinical
  name: Speech apraxia
  frequency: OCCASIONAL
  description: >
    Three of the thirteen patients in the founding cohort showed characteristics of
    speech apraxia — a motor-programming speech disorder distinct from the general
    speech delay recorded for the whole cohort. This matters clinically because apraxia
    responds to motor-speech therapy techniques rather than generic language therapy, so
    speech-language assessment in MED13 syndrome should screen for it specifically.
    Across the cohort, speech production was disproportionately impaired relative to
    language comprehension.

- category: Clinical
  name: Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy with mitochondrial dysfunction
  frequency: VERY_RARE
  description: >
    A single infant with a de novo p.Pro835Ser MED13 variant presented with infantile
    spasms, hypertrophic cardiomyopathy and hepatomegaly, with autopsy showing
    mitochondrial abnormalities and reduced respiratory chain complex activity in cardiac
    and hepatic tissue. This is the first link between a MED13 variant and systemic
    mitochondrial dysfunction, and it distinguishes a severe multi-organ tail from the
    mild, structural congenital heart phenotype seen in the rest of the cohort.
  phenotype_term:

      label: Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy

    supports: SUPPORT

- category: Clinical
  name: Severe developmental language disorder
  description: >
    MED13 has been recovered as a monogenic cause in an unbiased severe

- name: 17q23.2 800 kb deletion encompassing MED13
  description: >-
    De novo whole-gene deletion detected by array CGH in a child with moderate intellectual
    disability, short stature, mild dysmorphism and hearing loss. The authors attributed
    the cataract, hearing loss and semicircular canal dysplasia specifically to MED13
    haploinsufficiency, making this the independent CNV support for a dosage mechanism.
    Caveat: the deletion spans six genes and the patient also carried a supernumerary
    marker chromosome 17, so attribution to MED13 alone is the authors' inference rather
    than a demonstrated fact. Cataract and semicircular canal dysplasia are not curated as
    MED13 syndrome phenotypes on that basis.
  clinical_significance: PATHOGENIC
  type: deletion
  gene:
    preferred_term: MED13
    term:
      id: hgnc:22474
      label: MED13
  evidence:

genetic:
- name: MED13 heterozygous pathogenic variants
  association: Causative
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: MED13
    term:
      id: hgnc:22474
      label: MED13
  inheritance:
  - name: Autosomal Dominant
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:41195223
      reference_title: "A novel frameshift variant in the MED13 gene causing intellectual developmental disorder-61 in a Chinese family."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Intellectual developmental disorder type 61 (MRD61) is an extremely rare
        autosomal dominant disorder caused by variants in the MED13 gene.
      explanation: >-
        Confirms autosomal dominant inheritance pattern for MED13 syndrome.
  features: >
    MED13 syndrome is caused by heterozygous pathogenic variants in MED13.
    Most variants arise de novo, although parent-to-child transmission has
    been documented. Both truncating variants (frameshift, nonsense) causing
    haploinsufficiency and missense variants clustering at a conserved
    phosphodegron (p.Thr326/p.Pro327) and a C-terminal region have been
    identified. Six of 13 patients in the original cohort had truncating
    mutations and seven had non-truncating variants. To date approximately
    26 cases have been reported worldwide.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:29740699
    reference_title: "De novo mutations in MED13, a component of the Mediator complex, are associated with a novel neurodevelopmental disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Here we describe a GeneMatcher collaboration which led to a cohort of 13
      affected individuals harboring protein-altering variants, 11 of which are
      de novo, in MED13; the only inherited variant was transmitted to an
      affected child from an affected mother.
    explanation: >-
      Foundational paper establishing MED13 as a disease gene with predominantly
      de novo variants.
  - reference: PMID:29740699
    reference_title: "De novo mutations in MED13, a component of the Mediator complex, are associated with a novel neurodevelopmental disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Six affected individuals had mutations that are predicted to truncate the
      MED13 protein, six had missense mutations, and one had an
      in-frame-deletion of one amino acid. Out of the seven non-truncating
      mutations, six clustered in two specific locations of the MED13 protein:
      an N-terminal and C-terminal region.
    explanation: >-
      Describes the variant spectrum including both truncating and clustered
      missense variants.
  - reference: PMID:41195223
    reference_title: "A novel frameshift variant in the MED13 gene causing intellectual developmental disorder-61 in a Chinese family."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Intellectual developmental disorder type 61 (MRD61) is an extremely rare
      autosomal dominant disorder caused by variants in the MED13 gene. This
      gene encodes a subunit of the mediator complex, which is also known as
      TRAP, SMCC, DRIP or ARC. This complex functions as a transcriptional
      coactivator and is essential for the expression of almost all genes. To
      date, only 26 cases of MRD61 have been reported worldwide.
    explanation: >-
      Confirms rarity (26 cases) and autosomal dominant inheritance.
  - reference: PMID:41561257
    reference_title: "Intellectual Developmental Disorder of Autosomal Dominant 61 Caused by a MED13 Variant Presenting With Congenital Unilateral Sensorineural Hearing Loss: A Case Report."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      This case is also consistent with haploinsufficiency as the disease
      mechanism for truncating MED13 variants in MRD61, underscoring the
      importance of exome sequencing in patients with neurodevelopmental
      disorders and congenital anomalies.
    explanation: >-
      Confirms haploinsufficiency as the mechanism for truncating variants.

datasets:
- accession: "geo:GSE298801"
  title: Cardiomyocyte-specific Deletion of Med13 and Med13L Results in Dysregulated Gene Expression and Lethal Heart Failure
  description: >-
    Bulk RNA-seq from adult murine cardiomyocytes after inducible knockout of Med13
    and Med13L. Double knockout results in lethal heart failure within 6 weeks,
    with significant gene dysregulation of fibrotic pathways and calcium handling.
    Demonstrates that Med13 and Med13L function redundantly in the adult heart to
    maintain basal cardiac function and transcription, relevant to the congenital
    heart defects observed in MED13 syndrome.
  organism:
    preferred_term: mouse
    term:
      id: NCBITaxon:10090
      label: Mus musculus
  data_type: BULK_RNA_SEQ
  sample_count: 8
  sample_types:
  - preferred_term: cardiomyocyte
    tissue_term:
      preferred_term: heart
      term:
        id: UBERON:0000948
        label: heart
  conditions:
  - Med13/Med13L cardiomyocyte-specific double knockout
  - wild-type control
  genes:
  - preferred_term: MED13
    term:
      id: hgnc:22474
      label: MED13
  - preferred_term: MED13L
    term:
      id: hgnc:22962
      label: MED13L
  publication: PMID:40989238
  findings:
  - statement: Med13 and Med13L are functionally redundant in adult cardiomyocytes
  - statement: Double knockout causes lethal heart failure with fibrotic and calcium handling gene dysregulation
  - statement: Similar gene dysregulation patterns across Mediator cardiac knockouts (Med13/13L, Med12, Med1, Med30)
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40989238
    reference_title: "Med13 and Med13L: Critical redundant players in basal cardiac function and gene expression."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "Med13/13L knockout resulted in decreased cardiac function leading to lethal heart failure in a median timeframe of 6 weeks from the start of tamoxifen."
    explanation: Mouse model demonstrates functional redundancy of Med13 and Med13L in heart and lethal consequences of combined loss.

inheritance:
- name: Autosomal Dominant
  inheritance_term:
    preferred_term: Autosomal dominant inheritance
    term:
      id: HP:0000006
      label: Autosomal dominant inheritance
  penetrance: INCOMPLETE
  expressivity: VARIABLE
  description: >
    Autosomal dominant, with variants predominantly arising de novo. Two departures from
    a simple dominant model matter for counselling. First, penetrance is incomplete: a
    pathogenic MED13 variant has been documented in an affected proband inherited from an
    asymptomatic parent, in whom the variant had itself arisen de novo. Second, vertical
    transmission from an affected parent is documented in at least two families. A
    variant should therefore not be dismissed because a parent carries it and is
    unaffected, and parental testing is warranted even when the phenotype looks sporadic.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38453051
    reference_title: "Penetrance, variable expressivity and monogenic neurodevelopmental disorders."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The patients carried different variants (1 splice-site variant, 4 nonsense and 7
      frameshift) in 11 genes: CAMTA1, MBD5, KMT2C, KMT2E, ZMIZ1, MN1, NDUFB11, CUL3,
      MED13, ARID2 and RERE.
    explanation: >-
      MED13 appears in a systematically assembled series of neurodevelopmental-disorder
      genes with a pathogenic variant inherited from an asymptomatic parent.
  - reference: PMID:38453051
    reference_title: "Penetrance, variable expressivity and monogenic neurodevelopmental disorders."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Incomplete penetrance for SNV and MNV in neurodevelopmental disorders might be more
      frequent than previously thought. This point is crucial to consider for
      interpretation of variants, family investigation, genetic counseling, and prenatal
      diagnosis.
    explanation: >-
      States the counselling implication of incomplete penetrance for genes such as MED13
      that are usually treated as fully penetrant de novo disorders.
